1. PURPOSE
1.1 This fictional SOP defines how FTD numbers, approves, issues, reviews and retires controlled
    documents so that only the current approved revision is in use.

2. SCOPE
2.1 Applies to all controlled documents at FTD: SOPs, Work Instructions, Forms, Specifications and Policies.

3. DEFINITIONS
3.1 Controlled document: a document with a Document ID, revision and approval status.
3.2 Document register: the master list of controlled documents (Appendix A).
3.3 Effective date: the date from which a revision must be used.
3.4 Obsolete: a document withdrawn from use and no longer to be followed.

4. RESPONSIBILITIES
4.1 Author: drafts the document and proposes revisions.
4.2 Department Head: confirms technical accuracy and the owning department.
4.3 QA Reviewer: checks format, numbering and compliance with this SOP.
4.4 QA Head: gives final approval and sets the effective date.
4.5 Document Control Coordinator (in QA): maintains the register and issues approved copies.

5. NUMBERING AND REVISION
5.1 Document ID format: FTD-<TYPE>-<DEPT>-<NNNN>, where NNNN is a 4-digit sequence number
    that is unique within each TYPE + DEPT combination.
5.2 Document types (TYPE): SOP (Standard Operating Procedure), WI (Work Instruction), FRM (Form),
    SPC (Specification), POL (Policy).
5.3 Department codes (DEPT): QA Quality Assurance, QC Quality Control, MF Manufacturing,
    WH Warehouse, EN Engineering, PK Packaging, FC Facilities, HR Human Resources,
    IT Information Technology.
5.4 Revision format: "Rev NN" (two digits). The first issue is Rev 01 and each approved
    revision increases the number by 1. The Document ID never changes between revisions.

6. LIFECYCLE STATUS VALUES
6.1 DRAFT       : being written; not approved; no effective date.
6.2 IN_REVIEW   : circulated for review/approval; not approved; no effective date.
6.3 EFFECTIVE   : approved and in force.
6.4 OBSOLETE    : withdrawn from use (superseded or no longer needed).

7. APPROVAL AND EFFECTIVE DATE
7.1 Four approvals are required before a document can become EFFECTIVE: Author, Department Head,
    QA Reviewer and QA Head. Approval is complete on the date of the last signature
    (the Approved Date).
7.2 The Effective Date must be at least 7 days and at most 30 days after the Approved Date.
7.3 All affected employees must complete training on the new revision (100% completion)
    before the Effective Date.
7.4 Every controlled document shows: Document ID, Revision, Effective Date, Title and Approval Status.

8. PERIODIC REVIEW
8.1 Each EFFECTIVE document is reviewed on a fixed cycle counted from its Effective Date:
      SOP : every 24 months     WI  : every 24 months     FRM : every 36 months
      SPC : every 12 months     POL : every 36 months
8.2 The Next Review Date = Effective Date + the cycle for the document type.
8.3 A review is OVERDUE when the Next Review Date is earlier than today's date; the register shows
    Review_Overdue = Y. Overdue documents are reported to the QA Head monthly.

9. OBSOLESCENCE AND RETENTION
9.1 When a new revision becomes effective, the previous revision is withdrawn from all points of use
    within 1 business day.
9.2 Obsolete documents are retained in the archive for 7 years from the date they became obsolete.
9.3 Obsolete documents carry the status OBSOLETE and no Next Review Date.

10. REVISION HISTORY
    Rev 01 | 2020-01-06 | Initial issue
    Rev 02 | 2021-10-18 | Added Specification (SPC) and Policy (POL) document types
    Rev 03 | 2023-04-03 | Added 7-day minimum before effective date and training requirement
    Rev 04 | 2025-01-10 | Added review cycles per type, overdue rule and 1-business-day withdrawal rule
